# Seed Directory — Filters, Seller Batch & Phase 3 Report

Covers: Phase 2 (season / sowing-method / hue filters, Seed Directory rename, packet-card revision, Hues dahlia icons), the seller batch (imported Google ratings, state shapes, state filter, state pages, header-logo fix), and Phase 3 (accounts + waiting lists).

Production: https://seeds.hues.flowers · Worker `seeds-hues` · D1 `seeds-hues`

---

## Phase 2 — Filters, rename, cards, icons (LIVE 2026-11-17 ~04:10 UTC)

**Classification data (sourced only, never guessed):**
- Season: 81/93 crops classified — 48 cool, 33 warm (314 cool / 273 warm / 32 unclassified varieties). Sources: The Gardener's Workshop published collections (33 cool / 12 warm crops) + Floret Library Sowing & Growing text (36 crops). 12 crops left unclassified (incl. Ornamental Grass — listed in both TGW collections; Pea — mixed Floret types; Virginia Stock — no records). Basis per crop in `season-classification.json`.
- Sowing method (multi-value, from Floret text): 91/93 crops — 60 transplant-only, 27 both, 4 direct-only (Breadseed Poppy, Annual Baby's Breath, Bee's Friend, Pea); unclassified: Virginia Stock, Eucalyptus. File: `method-classification.json`.
- Hue (variety-level, 12 families + mix): 564/619 varieties (91%) — white 85, purple 74, pink 71, peach 59, mix 56, green 54, yellow 34, orange 29, blue 27, red 23, chocolate 20, blush 18, burgundy 14; 55 unknown. File: `variety-colors.json`.
- Filters combine (season + method + hue) in one BrowseFilters UI with swatch dots plus text labels; invalid values return 400. Live example: `/variety?season=cool&method=direct` → "68 varieties · Cool Season Hardy Annuals · Direct Sow".
- Migrations 0005 (plants.season_class) + 0006 (plant_sowing_methods, 118 rows) applied to production D1.

**Rename:** the product is "Seed Directory" everywhere user-visible (header/footer wordmark, APP_NAME, PWA manifest, page titles); domain and URL paths unchanged; bot UA intentionally left as HuesSeedFinderBot. Live homepage contains "Seed Directory" and zero "Seed Finder".

**Cards:** the packet-card imprint line was removed entirely; crop name moved to the bottom, centered, in a fixed-height name zone; all 93 cards measured exactly 594px (desktop) / 396px (mobile). The visible "cropping" was the card frame forcing a 1:2 thumb into aspect-[4/5] with object-cover (cutting ~37%) — fixed to aspect-[1/2] + object-contain on paper; the served image files themselves were never cropped (non-1:2 masters had been padded with their own background colour at publish). Illustration standard set at 1120×2240 (1:2), pad-never-crop, recorded in `artwork-crops/SOURCES.md` and `artwork-pilot/SOURCES.md`; audit: 93/93 heroes 1120×2240, 93/93 thumbs 480×960, 6/6 variety pilots compliant — nothing needed regeneration.

**Header:** Hues logo enlarged 22px → 44px desktop / 36px mobile (header height unchanged at 57px); About removed from header and mobile nav (kept in footer).

**Icons:** zinnia set replaced with the Hues dahlia mark on paper #F8F1E1 (192/512/maskable/apple-touch/SVG/favicon.ico); manifest, layout, service-worker template and theme-color updated; regeneration via `scripts/gen-icons.py` / `npm run icons`; all icon URLs live-verified 200.

**Deploy:** Worker version `63e052fc-a11a-4ed6-b7f4-44975d446e17` (rollback target `a60980f1-ac10-46e4-b8eb-27d17222790f`); catalog v5 published 2026-11-17T04:08:40Z (snapshot sha256 `edf4a3e7cf4baa0e3e9727d84e6d79e14e3776743c7b252a649a481d05d00c21`); 749 paths revalidated, all 200; `npm run verify` exit 0, Vitest 167/167, ESLint + tsc clean.
*(Note, found 2026-11-17 during the seller-batch deploy: the v5 snapshot object had only been uploaded to the local R2 state — the live snapshot endpoint 404'd for v5. Fixed in the seller batch below by re-uploading with `wrangler r2 object put --remote`; v5 endpoint now serves 200, byte-identical.)*

## Seller batch — summaries, imported Google ratings, state shapes/pages, header-logo fix (LIVE 2026-11-17)

**History:** the original Kiro run for this batch (brief 4) was terminated (SIGTERM, ~27 min) before verification. On Dustin's go ("Fix the blocked seller batch next, then Phase 3", 2026-11-17), the partial state was inspected file-by-file first: it proved substantially complete and correct — migration 0007, StateShape/StateFilter/SellerCard/ImportedRatingPanel components, `lib/data/states.ts` + `state-paths.ts`, `/state/[slug]` route, seller-service region filter, validators, sync/snapshot plumbing, `scripts/add-reviews-data.py`, and unit/API/acceptance tests were all present. Decision: keep all files; the full verify suite passed untouched (Vitest 167/167 at inspection; 188/189 on re-run — the one failure is a pre-existing rate-limit contract test timing out at 60s under sandbox CPU contention, unrelated to this batch; it passed in the inspection run).

**Data (ready before the code):**
- Seller summaries for all 25 sellers, written in our own words from each farm's own site / exemplar dossiers (`seller-summaries.json`), live since the Phase 2 deploy. Three location corrections applied in the same pass: Johnny's → Winslow, ME; Sunflower Selections → Woodland, CA; Three Brothers Blooms → Camano Island, WA. All 25 sellers have a region; 16 states represented.
- Google reviews collected via the sanctioned Places route only (no scraping): place IDs for 14/25 sellers, aggregate ratings for 7 (`seller-reviews.json`): Hudson Valley 5.0/12, Botanical Interests 4.8/72, Prairie Moon 4.7/136, Johnny's 4.7/313, 3 Porch Farm 5.0/5, High Mowing 4.7/1014, Seed Savers Exchange 4.8/1035. The Places route exposes aggregate rating + count ONLY — no attributable review text exists, and none was invented.
- State outline assets: simplified single-path SVGs generated from Census-derived GeoJSON for all 16 seller states (`state-shapes/`), leaf-deep stroke, aria-hidden, state name always adjacent as text.

**Shipped in this batch:**
- Migration `0007_seller_imported_rating.sql` applied to production D1 (additive, six nullable columns on sellers, CHECK-constrained; our own rating fields untouched).
- Imported ratings written to production for the 7 sellers (targeted SQL via `wrangler d1 execute --remote`, values verified by read-back) and merged into `seed-data/catalog.json` by `scripts/add-reviews-data.py`.
- "Reviews from Google" panel on seller pages: bordered panel below the summary, aggregate as text + stars with accessible label ("Rated 4.7 out of 5"), "from N reviews on Google", attribution "Ratings from Google · fetched 2026-11-16". Kept strictly separate from our own rating, which still reads "Not yet rated"; the imported number is not in JSON-LD or any of our rating fields.
- StateShape icon beside the location on seller pages (icon + state name linking to the state page) and on SellerCards (plain icon + text inside the card's single link — no nested links).
- `/seller` state filter: chips with shape + name + count, only states with ≥1 seller, single-select via `?state=OR`, server-side through the sellers service.
- `/state/{slug}` pages for exactly the 16 states with sellers: H1 "Seed farms in {State}", large state outline, factual count intro, SellerCard grid for exactly that state's sellers, indexable, in the sitemap (16 state URLs). States without farms 404, including real states (`/state/texas` → 404). Per the brief, no Add Your Farm button in this batch — it ships with Phase 3 (added there; see below).
- **Header-logo fix (Dustin's flag):** the header Hues logo now links to `/` (Seed Directory home), aria-label "Seed Directory — home". The footer attribution line keeps its https://hues.flowers link, unchanged.

**Deploy mechanics (sandbox constraints, for the record):** the Miniflare remote-D1 proxy path cannot cross this sandbox's egress proxy, and the OpenNext R2 cache-population step cannot reach R2's S3 endpoint — both pre-existing, documented in INDEX-RULING-REPORT.md. The deploy therefore used: targeted SQL via the wrangler CLI for data; the snapshot built locally with the project's own `buildSnapshotFile()` (its internal catalog_publish row patched to version 6), uploaded with `wrangler r2 object put --remote`, publish row inserted via SQL; the Worker deployed with plain `wrangler deploy` from an isolated staging copy of the OpenNext build (staging config reproduced the production bindings verbatim). D1 export as a backup is blocked by FTS5 virtual tables; the catalog v5 snapshot + additive-only changes served as the safety net.

**Result:** Worker version `a80e1ddf-f00c-4c80-a1cc-30b49f57aa18` (rollback target: `63e052fc-a11a-4ed6-b7f4-44975d446e17` code, deployed as `41527cb7-d3dc-443b-97d6-ecfb59421b08`); **catalog v6** published 2026-11-17T07:02:54Z, snapshot `snapshots/catalog-v6.sqlite` sha256 `ca4f8412c473811ae8cde79bb7e7e8b1814020fa735367149daea9052b2bfdb9` — the file served at `/api/v1/catalog/snapshot/6.sqlite` verified byte-identical.

**Live verification (production, 2026-11-17):**
- https://seeds.hues.flowers/state/oregon → 200, lists exactly the 3 Oregon sellers (Peace Seedlings, Summertime Seed Co., Wild West Seed).
- https://seeds.hues.flowers/state/texas → 404.
- https://seeds.hues.flowers/seller/johnnys-selected-seeds → "Reviews from Google" panel present (`data-imported-rating="Google"`, API `importedRating` 4.7/313) and still "Not yet rated"; https://seeds.hues.flowers/seller/hudson-valley-seed-co → panel with aria-label "Rated 5.0 out of 5", "from 12 reviews on Google", "Ratings from Google · fetched 2026-11-16", still "Not yet rated".
- Homepage header logo anchor: `href="/"` with aria-label "Seed Directory — home".
- https://seeds.hues.flowers/seller?state=VT → exactly the 3 Vermont sellers (American Meadows, High Mowing Organic Seeds, Jig-Bee Flower Farm); https://seeds.hues.flowers/state/vermont → 200.
- Sitemap contains 16 `/state/` URLs; `/api/v1/catalog/version` reports version 6.

## Phase 3 — Accounts + waiting lists (LIVE 2026-11-17 — resumed and finished on Dustin's go)

**History:** the Phase 3 Kiro build ran ~117 minutes and produced a substantial, coherent implementation, but the session was terminated (SIGTERM) when an approval prompt went unanswered past its timeout, before the final verify/acceptance pass and any deploy. Dustin then approved resuming ("DUSTIN'S GO on Phase 3 — resume approved", 2026-11-17); the coordinator reviewed the build file-by-file against brief 3 + its 7 addenda, ran the full verify, configured what could be configured, deployed it with the Step A illustration deploy (Worker `414d6869-2924-438f-bfce-6639310d5e71`), and live-verified it (below).

**What exists in the tree (inspected, not yet verified end-to-end):**
- Migration `0008_accounts_waitlists.sql`: extends the Phase 1 `users` stub (name, phone, zip, usda_zone, email_verified(+at), notify_opt_out, status, farm_owner_declared, is_farm_owner, seller_id, alert_phone(+verified), sms_opt_in(+at)) and creates 8 tables (sessions, verification codes, waitlist entries, notification outbox, farm submissions, farm-owner audit, + support tables) — additive only, all private (excluded from snapshot/sync/API by design).
- Libraries: `lib/turnstile.ts`, `lib/mail.ts`, `lib/sms.ts` (SMS_PROVIDER switch, Twilio-first + mock), `lib/session.ts`, `lib/accounts.ts`, services for accounts / verification (shared 6-digit code machinery, hashed+salted, 30-min expiry, 5 attempts, resend invalidates) / waitlist matcher / notifications drain / farm-summary drafting with evidence-quote validation; repositories for accounts, waitlist, farm submissions.
- ZIP→zone: self-hosted `lib/data/zip-zones.json` + `lib/data/zones.ts` (generated by `scripts/gen-zip-zones.ts`).
- UI/routes: `/account` (create/sign-in forms, Alerts section), `/add-your-farm` (locked field list), variety-page WaitlistBlock, header account icon + "Add Your Farm" CTA, state pages gained the Add Your Farm CTA → `/add-your-farm?state={code}` (Addendum 5), `/admin/waitlist`, `/unsubscribe`, Twilio SMS webhook route, scheduled-drain job (`lib/jobs/scheduled.ts`).
- Tests added: `tests/integration/accounts.test.ts`, `tests/integration/farm-summary.test.ts` (+ local farm-site fixtures), `tests/unit/account-privacy.test.ts`, `tests/unit/accounts-lib.test.ts`, acceptance-suite extensions.
- Spot check after the interruption: `tsc --noEmit` reported no errors. The full `npm run verify` + acceptance suite did **not** complete, and no test users were created in production (nothing was deployed).

**Finish pass (2026-11-17, coordinator):**
- Review: migration 0008, provider libraries, header, forms, env handling inspected against brief 3 + addenda. Confirmed: `ADMIN_NOTIFY_EMAIL` is env-only (null = notices skipped, never hardcoded); the Add Your Farm business-phone field is required and carries no prefill from the account phone; SMS with no provider configured leaves rows queued and reports `sms_not_configured`; Turnstile fails closed (no token → 403).
- `npm run verify` green twice: ESLint clean, tsc clean, **Vitest 245/245** (incl. `tests/integration/accounts.test.ts`). Kiro's local acceptance reached **116/117**; the one failure is the documented rate-limit window-boundary flake in the local environment.
- Migration `0008_accounts_waitlists.sql` **applied to production D1** (`wrangler d1 migrations apply --remote`, 40 commands).
- **Configured:** a **real Turnstile widget** was created in Dustin's Cloudflare account for seeds.hues.flowers (site key in `wrangler.jsonc` vars; secret set as a Worker secret — no test keys anywhere near production), and `UNSUBSCRIBE_SIGNING_KEY` was generated and set as a Worker secret. The deploy also registered the 15-minute notification-drain cron from `wrangler.jsonc`.
- **Live verification (production):** `/account` → 200 with create + sign-in forms, the real Turnstile site key and the ZIP field; `/add-your-farm?state=OR` → 200 with OR prefilled and the full locked field list (incl. business_phone, tiktok_url); `POST /api/v1/account` without a Turnstile token → **403 `turnstile_failed`**; `POST /api/v1/internal/notifications/drain` without an admin key → **401**; waitlist panel present on a zero-seller variety page (`data-testid="waitlist-block"`); `/admin/waitlist` → 200; state pages carry the `/add-your-farm?state={code}` CTA.
- **Test-user rule:** production account tables checked after deploy — **users 0, waitlist_entries 0, farm_submissions 0, sessions 0, notification_outbox 0**. No test account was ever created in production (a real Turnstile token requires a browser, and email delivery is not yet configured); local acceptance fixtures lived only in the local D1. Nothing to delete.
- **Not yet live-testable:** the full signup → email-code → sign-in → waitlist-join journey needs a real browser for the Turnstile widget and working email delivery (below). It is covered by the integration tests and the local acceptance run.

**Remaining blockers — Dustin's hands:**
1. **Cloudflare Email Sending onboarding** for seeds.hues.flowers (Dashboard → Email Service → Email Sending → Onboard Domain), then uncomment the `send_email` binding in `wrangler.jsonc` and redeploy. Until then verification-code and alert emails cannot send; the drain reports `mail_not_configured` and rows stay queued.
2. **Twilio:** create the account, register a sender (toll-free verification), then set secrets `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID` / `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` and vars `SMS_PROVIDER=twilio`, `SMS_SENDER_ID`; point Twilio's inbound webhook at `https://seeds.hues.flowers/api/v1/webhooks/twilio/sms`. Until then SMS alerts stay queued (`sms_not_configured`).
3. **`ADMIN_NOTIFY_EMAIL`:** choose the address for admin notices (new farm submissions, farm-owner declarations) and set it as a Worker var + redeploy.

## Illustration deploys + mobile TabBar fix (LIVE 2026-11-17)

Step A deploy: Worker `414d6869-2924-438f-bfce-6639310d5e71` (rollback target recorded in `v2/tmp/stepA-rollback-target.txt`: `88fdc4a7-9527-4c65-a137-d55828896cdd`, catalog v6). Step C deploy: Worker `de79991f-b605-4c31-a352-aebeaf6f641d` (current).

- **557 variety illustrations wired in** following the pilot pattern exactly (files in `v2/public/artwork/<slug>.webp`; media rows whose `r2_key` is the public path, inserted directly into production D1 as with the pilots/crops — note: raw-SQL inserts must supply `created_at`, which has an app-level default only). Production media after Step C: **566 variety rows** (557 + 6 pilots + 3 retries), 659 total.
- **Optimization pass (Dustin's addendum):** served copies re-encoded from the PNG masters (masters untouched) at WebP q80 / method 6, metadata stripped. Heroes: 146.9 MB → **95.6 MB total; median 258 KB → 167 KB; max 436 KB → 285 KB** (one file, Chocolate Lace Flower 'Dara', finished at q78 — the stated floor — to land under 300 KB). Thumbnails (480×960; not site-served for varieties): median 56 KB → 46 KB — the ~35 KB target was not chased below the q78–80 sharpness floor. A 768-wide responsive variant (`/artwork/w768/`, median 98 KB) ships for all 566 variety heroes via a lightweight `srcset` in `VarietyHeroImage` (hero displays at ≤380 CSS px, so 768w covers 2× DPR).
- **Sharpness verified visually** on 3 re-encoded heroes against their masters (Shirley Poppy 'Mother of Pearl'; Chocolate Lace Flower 'Dara' at q78; Amaranth 'Hot Biscuits'): petal veining, stamen detail, fine foliage and paper grain preserved; no banding, no edge mush.
- **Retries (Dustin-authorized "deploy then retry, then deploy again"):** `zinnia-little-flower-girl`, `marigold-la-ribera`, `ornamental-squash-american-tondo` regenerated from their Floret reference photos in the established Print Edition watercolor style, visually inspected against the references before acceptance, normalized to 1120×2240 via `normalize.py`, optimized identically, manifest updated (`update-manifest.py`). Manifest now: **560 done, 6 pilot-done, 52 no-reference, 1 reference-uncertain, 0 pending**.
- **Live verification:** Step A — 5 variety pages across different crops (Shirley Poppy, Sweet Pea, Zinnia, Amaranth, Annual Baby's Breath) render hero + w768 srcset; the no-reference page `/variety/snapdragon-rembrandt` returns 200 with no artwork image. Step C — all 3 retried pages render their illustration (HTTP 200 at optimized sizes: 192 KB / 290 KB / 249 KB).
- **Mobile TabBar fix (Dustin's rider, "Varieties page is missing from mobile"):** the mobile bottom bar is now **five tabs — Home / Crops / Varieties / Farms / Search** (`grid-cols-5`, ≥44pt targets, new `BloomIcon` in the existing line-icon set). Crops match narrowed to `/crop` + `/series` (it previously swallowed `/variety`); Varieties matches `/variety`. Desktop header nav unchanged. Live-verified in production HTML: on `/variety/*` the Varieties tab carries `aria-current="page"` + active styling and the Crops tab does not highlight; on `/crop/*` Crops is current and Varieties is not; Farms is current on `/seller`.

## State-flower header art (LIVE 2026-11-17)

Dustin: "Put a beautiful illustration including the state flower inside the state shape — for every state." Artwork (16 paintings, one per seller state, each featuring the official state flower; masters + provenance in `artwork-states/` with MANIFEST.json/SOURCES.md) integrated into the state-page headers:

- **Component:** `StateShape` gained an opt-in `art` prop (default rendering byte-identical — the small icons on seller cards and beside locations are unchanged, live-verified: 40 state SVGs on /seller, none containing an image). With `art`, the artwork at `/artwork/states/{slug}.webp` renders inside an SVG `clipPath` built from the existing state path geometry (deterministic clip id `state-art-clip-{CODE}`, SSR-safe), the image covering the shape's bounding box with `preserveAspectRatio="xMidYMid slice"`, and the leaf-deep outline redrawn over the top (`fill="none"`, same stroke settings) so the silhouette stays crisp. Still `aria-hidden` decorative with the state name as adjacent text; no caption or credit (per the sitewide caption removal).
- **Assets:** 800×800 WebP q80/method 6 derived from the 1600×1600 PNG masters (masters untouched), served from the app's own public assets at `/artwork/states/<slug>.webp` — 73–124 KB each, 1.5 MB total for all 16.
- **Verify:** `npm run verify` green (ESLint + tsc clean, Vitest 248/248 incl. new art-variant unit tests); acceptance **119/119** against the local preview of the deployed build (the /state/oregon check now also asserts the artwork href + clip id). Screenshots eyeballed at desktop + 390px: `theme-preview-shots/state-art-{ca,or}-{desktop,mobile}.png` — flower reads clearly inside the silhouette, outline crisp, no overflow.
- **Deploy:** Worker **`5b7f974a-5bee-4fe1-b0c7-b0b988274aea`**. Note for future deploys: `wrangler deploy` run from inside v2/ is intercepted by OpenNext ("OpenNext project detected") and dies in the R2 cache-population step; the working form is wrangler run from a neutral directory with `-c ~/workspace/seeds-hues/v2/wrangler.jsonc` (paths resolve against the config), via `tmp/cf-run.py` for the token.
- **Live verification:** /state/california, /state/oregon, /state/washington all 200 with the clipped artwork markup; the three artwork files serve 200 at their on-disk sizes; seller pages and cards keep the plain line icons.

## Source-attribution removal (LIVE 2026-11-17)

Dustin: "Remove notes in parentheses i.e. per The Gardener's Workshop, per Floret Library, etc. Remove any attribution to sources of information on public site." Display-layer only — stored provenance, the API fields (`season_source_label`, `sowing_method_source_label`, listing `sourceLabel`/`provenance`), ingest and the review queue are untouched (integration tests asserting the API still returns the labels stay green).

- **Removed from rendering:** the `(per …)` parentheticals on crop-page Season / Sowing method lines; the "Source: …" spans under the Season and Sowing method facts on variety pages; the " · Source: {host}" segment on listing rows (rows now show only "Last checked {date}" — Shop still goes through /go).
- **Copy made truthful in the same pass:** the home card and About-the-data section were retitled "A checked date on every listing" (the API-provenance sentence on About-the-data kept verbatim — the API is unchanged); three more public strings that claimed listings show a source were corrected — the site footer, `SITE_DESCRIPTION` (meta description), and llms.txt. Deliberately LEFT: the Terms page's data-use clause ("keep each listing together with its source and check date when you display it") — it governs API consumers, and the API still returns provenance.
- **Exception (Dustin confirmed):** the "Reviews from Google" panel stays exactly as is (Google terms requirement) — live-verified unchanged on Johnny's (4.7, 313 reviews, "Ratings from Google · fetched 2026-11-16"), still separate from "Not yet rated". The seller *named* The Gardener's Workshop is a listed farm, not an attribution — its pages are untouched.
- **Verify:** `npm run verify` green (248/248); acceptance **120/120** (new negative checks: /crop/zinnia and the King's Ransom variety page assert the facts render with NO "(per ", "Source:", "Floret Library", "Gardener's Workshop", "Sowing & Growing Notes"). Deploys: main batch Worker `8e4f6eb4-a04a-46b9-a9f8-7e5885add9f6`; copy follow-up Worker `9a0d70d7-496d-4d4b-a8d7-f0b523c078cb` (current production).
- **Live verification:** /crop/zinnia and /crop/sweet-pea show clean lines ("Season: Warm Season Tender Annuals", "Sowing method: Direct Sow · Start in Plug Trays"); variety pages (King's Ransom, Queen Lime Blush) contain zero attribution strings; footer, llms.txt and the meta description carry the corrected wording.

## 2026-11-18 — Image migration to R2 + Image Transformations COMPLETE (LIVE)

Dustin approved moving all served images to Cloudflare R2 with on-the-fly sizing: one full-quality original per image in R2, the app requests width/quality/format in the URL, Cloudflare generates and caches that version. A first coordinator run was cancelled mid-flight on 2026-11-18; Dustin directed "Verify what made it and send the remainder." Verification found the uploads and the CDN code deploy had in fact completed; the remainder was the local-file retirement, its deploy, and the GitHub push.

- **R2 verification (bucket `seeds-hues-images`, served at images.seeds.hues.flowers):** listed against `image-migration/manifest.json` — **758 expected / 758 actual / 0 missing / 0 extra / 0 size mismatches** (every object's byte size matches its original). Derived variants (w480/w768/crop thumbs) were deliberately NOT uploaded; they are transformations now. `/brand/*`, `/icons/*` and favicon stay local static by design; reference photos and hidden files were never uploaded.
- **Code (Claude Code):** new `lib/images.ts` (`originalKey`/`imageUrl`/`imageSrcSet`; URL shape `https://images.seeds.hues.flowers/cdn-cgi/image/width=<w>,quality=80,format=auto,fit=scale-down/<key>` — scale-down ONLY, paintings shown whole, never cropped, never upscaled; SVGs bypass transformations) + generated `lib/images-data.ts` (759 stored paths → R2 keys; `npm run images:data` regenerates). All rendering routed through the helper (variety/crop cards + heroes, state shapes + scenes, theme art, pod dividers, OG images). Stored paths stay canonical in D1/API (`media.url` unchanged); `thumbUrl` no longer computed. Acceptance's old local-file check for `/artwork/zinnia-queen-lime-blush.webp` was retired in the same change and replaced with CDN-original + transformation checks. `v2/README.md` artwork sections updated to the R2 pipeline.
- **Verify:** `npm run verify` green on the final tree (ESLint + tsc clean, Vitest **334/334**); acceptance **134/134** for this code (incl. the new CDN checks: original 200 image/webp 1120×2240 immutable; transformations serve the requested width whole, never upscale; SVG served as stored).
- **Remainder deploy:** fresh `cf:build` (assets 2,053 → **91 files**, no `artwork/` directory) + deploy, Worker **`c9745990-4e6d-4841-b32c-dcc619446b6e`**. The 1,962 retired files: 566 variety + 566 w480 + 566 w768 + 248 crops/thumbs + 16 states under `public/artwork/`.
- **Live verification (post-deploy):** home, /crop/zinnia and the Queen Lime Blush variety page 200; retired paths now 404 (`/artwork/zinnia-queen-lime-blush.webp`, `/artwork/w480/…`, `/artwork/crops/zinnia.webp`); CDN original + width-480 transformation 200. Full sweep of 15 page types: **191 unique image URLs, all 200, zero local `/artwork/` references in HTML**; the only non-CDN img is `/brand/hues-logo.svg` (local by design). Theme assets the CSS still references locally (`/theme/paper-grain.webp`, `/theme/divider-stem.svg`), favicon and icons all 200.
- **Acceptance against production note:** a full acceptance run pointed at the production base scores 95/123 — every failure traces to the suite's local-D1 assumptions (keys minted in local D1, local seed counts of 17 listings, local media-emptiness premises, local catalog_seq), not to this deploy; the suite's gate is the local-preview run above. Production data (319 listings, catalog v9) is untouched by this migration.
- **GitHub:** the migration (code + new image modules/tests + the 1,962-file retirement) committed and pushed to `main` on github.com/dustinboling/seeds-hues, on top of the initial backup commit.

## 2026-11-18 — False listing corrected (id 288) + crop-collision audit

Dustin reported in person that the listing on Breadseed Poppy 'Black Beauty' linked to a sunflower. Verified firsthand: listing **288** (Sunflower Selections) pointed to https://www.sunflowerselections.com/product/black-beauty/ — page title **"Black Beauty Sunflowers"**; the product is a pollenless branching SUNFLOWER variety (described alongside Terracotta, Moulin Rouge, Chianti, ProCut Red), not Papaver somniferum. Crawl Pass 1's ingest had matched it as an "exact" match (confidence 1) on the variety name "Black Beauty" alone, without checking the crop. It was the variety's only listing.

- **Retire (Priya, Catalog/Data):** same targeted production D1 pattern as the duplicate retire — one `catalog_seq` bump (2451 → 2452), `status='removed'` + `updated_at` + `updated_seq=2452`, and a `listing_events` row `event='gone'` at 2026-11-19T04:50:23Z. Row NOT deleted; event trail now reads first_seen → gone. **Verified via production:** `GET /api/v1/varieties/breadseed-poppy-black-beauty` → `listings: []`, `listingCount: 0`; the variety page shows "No sellers yet" with zero references to the seller domain; `/go/288` → 404.
- **Collision audit (same failure mode — matched product is a different crop than the variety's plant):**
  - **Sunflower Selections (all 3 of its listings):** 288 false (above); 330 ProCut White Lite and 331 ProCut White Nite both verified genuine from their product pages (titles "ProCut® White Lite Sunflowers" / "ProCut® White Nite Sunflowers", sunflower varieties on sunflower pages).
  - **Single-crop specialist sellers:** Sunflower Steve Seed Co. (sunflower) and Dawn Creek Farm (zinnia) — zero crop mismatches among visible listings. 3 Porch Farm checked separately (31 listings across 20 crops; no mum/chrysanthemum product URLs) — generalist in practice, no suspects.
  - **Crop-keyword scan of product URLs across all 393 visible listings:** 8 flags, all false positives — five are Shirley Poppy listings whose own crop keyword is "poppy" (ids 17, 149, 352, 353, 386), three are Johnny's listings where "Lavender" is a colour word and the URL itself names the correct crop (391 China Aster, 400/402 Delphinium).
  - **Cross-crop name collisions in the full catalog:** 9 variety names exist under two crops (alba, dawn creek blush, earl grey, pastel mix, pink, rembrandt, rose queen, sunset mix, white frills). The 4 with visible listings produced 6 listings, each fetched and verified genuine from page title/Latin name: 77 Floret Zinnia Dawn Creek Blush (*Zinnia elegans*), 118 Hudson Valley Rembrandt Snapdragon (*Antirrhinum majus*), 131 Jig-Bee Foxglove Alba (*Digitalis purpurea*), 216 Select Seeds Cup and Saucer Vine 'Alba' (*Cobaea scandens*), 222 Select Seeds Snapdragon 'Rembrandt', 356 Select Seeds Cleome 'Rose Queen' (*Cleome hassleriana*).
  - **Result: exactly ONE confirmed false listing (288), now retired. No borderline cases held — every suspect resolved to genuine from product-page evidence, never from name similarity.**
- **Counts reconcile:** visible listings 394 (319 crawl + 73 daily hunt + 2 auto-published) → **393** after the retire.
- **Systemic recommendation (engineering queue — NOT implemented here):** exact-match auto-publish must require CROP AGREEMENT whenever the product source identifies its crop: the matcher should extract a crop signal from the product page/feed (page title, Latin name, category/taxonomy, or a specialist seller's declared crop) and refuse auto-publish — queueing for review instead — when that signal names a different plant than the variety's plant, or when the seller is a known single-crop specialist and the variety's crop differs. Variety-name equality alone is not sufficient evidence for an exact match.

## 2026-11-18 — Seller page 500 fixed: D1 bound-parameter cap in packetRows + publicListingRows (LIVE)

Flagged during the motif-B deploy live checks: `/seller/select-seeds` returned 500 while other seller pages rendered 200. Reproduced, then diagnosed from Worker tail logs — no guessing.

- **Root cause (evidence):** `packetRows()` in `lib/repositories/catalog-read.ts` bound EVERY listing id in one `inArray(...)`. Select Seeds is the first seller to pass 100 visible listings (**103**), and D1 caps a statement at 100 bound parameters — the statement died with `too many SQL variables` (reproduced against production D1 with the exact query + params: 103 params → SQLITE_ERROR; 50 params → success). The seller API endpoint 500ed identically (same service path); the seller's own rows and listings are all well-formed — a code bug, not bad data. Every sibling loader in the same file (media loaders, coverageByPlant, the summary ids path) already chunks id lists at 50 for exactly this cap; packetRows (added with the packet-size display work) was missed.
- **Second instance found live during diagnosis (same defect family, fixed in the same change):** `GET /api/v1/recent?limit=100` also 500ed — `recent()` passes up to 100 listing ids into `publicListingRows()`, which bound them all on top of the query's ~6 fixed binds. limit=24 was fine; limit=100 was not.
- **Fix:** `packetRows()` dedupes ids and queries in chunks of 50 (chunks partition listing ids, so per-listing packet order — the only ordering its consumer `groupPackets()` relies on — is preserved). `publicListingRows()` is now a wrapper: >50 ids are fetched per-50-id chunks via the renamed `publicListingRowsQuery()` and merged with `listingComparator()`, a mirror of the SQL ORDER BY in both branches, following the file's summary-loader precedent; the wrapper also dedupes ids (Quinn's hardening note). ≤50 ids: single query, byte-equivalent behaviour. No price field is selected or surfaced by either change (R1 intact).
- **Bench note:** the Claude Code bench was at its 5-hour usage limit when the fix was ready; the coordinator applied the fix directly, mirroring the file's established chunking pattern line-for-line, and Quinn independently re-verified fail-on-old-code for both tests (restored HEAD, watched both fail with the production error, restored the fix byte-identical).
- **Regression tests (both in `tests/integration/data.test.ts`):** (1) 103 listings × 2 packet sizes for Songbird through the real `getSeller()` path — asserts count, presence, and per-listing packet content/order, cleanup in `finally`; fails on pre-fix code with `D1_ERROR: too many SQL variables at offset 417`. (2) 90 inserted listings + first_seen events (107 distinct ids) through `svc().recent({limit: 200})`; fails on pre-fix code at offset 1731 on the listings query itself.
- **Verify:** `npm run verify` green on the final tree (ESLint + tsc clean, Vitest **340/340** — 338 baseline + 2 new). Quinn gate: **PASS** (diff correctness, comparator fidelity for both orderings, caller sanity — packetRows and publicListingRows each have exactly one service caller — and no-leak cleanup all independently checked).
- **Deploy:** Worker **`a64e794c-d6c7-4f75-bf79-36b3d09b2c7f`** (Tessa's recipe: cf:build, wrangler from a neutral directory via `tmp/cf-run.py`).
- **Live verification:** `/seller/select-seeds` → **200** (was 500), API seller → 200 with all **103 listings** returned and rendered on the page (Ocean Pearls et al.); `/api/v1/recent?limit=100` → **200** (was 500); Songbird and The Gardener's Workshop seller pages still 200. Note: Select Seeds has 0 packet-size rows in production data (only 17 exist DB-wide, from seed data) — packet labels will appear for its listings when the queued packet-size population lands; that is data state, not part of this bug.
- **Follow-up watch:** any seller crossing 100 listings was on the same clock (next largest: The Gardener's Workshop, 53) — now structurally safe. Remaining unchunked `inArray` sites in catalog-read bind only small fixed vocabularies or caller-bounded filter lists, except the variety/listings `ids` filters, whose callers chunk or cap before reaching them (summary loader chunks >50; recent was the only unbounded ids caller and is now chunked at the repository).

## 2026-11-18 — Report form Turnstile never rendered: legacy implicit widget + page read an unset build-time site key (LIVE)

Dustin reported from his iPhone: submitting the /report form failed with the raw error "turnstile_token is required" and NO Turnstile widget visible anywhere on the form. Reproduced and diagnosed from the code + production env — two stacked defects, both on /report only:

- **Root cause 1 (widget pattern):** `components/report/ReportForm.tsx` was the ONLY form not using the shared widget — it inlined the legacy IMPLICIT Turnstile pattern (raw `<script src="https://challenges.cloudflare.com/turnstile/v0/api.js">` in component JSX + a `<div className="cf-turnstile">`) and read the token from FormData. Per Cloudflare's current docs, implicit rendering scans once at page load and suits static pages; explicit rendering (`api.js?render=explicit` + `turnstile.render()`) is the recommended pattern for SPAs/dynamic forms. In the React shell the scan could miss the container → no widget mounts → no `cf-turnstile-response` field → token silently omitted → server (`lib/services/reports.ts`) threw 400 `turnstile_required`, and the old form printed the API `detail` verbatim — the raw string Dustin saw.
- **Root cause 2 (site key sourcing — found by live verification of the first deploy):** `app/report/page.tsx` passed `siteKey={process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY}` — a build-time var that is NOT set in production — so the key was always null live and no widget could ever render on /report for anyone (the old form's own comment admitted it "renders only when NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY is set at build time"). The working forms' pages (account, add-your-farm) source the key via `getRuntimeEnv()` → `runtimeEnvFrom()`, which reads the `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` Worker var (set in wrangler.jsonc). The first Turnstile deploy (Worker `43e6838f`) fixed the form wiring but served the shared widget's "human check isn't set up on this server yet" note — live HTML inspection caught it; the page fix followed in the same ship cycle.
- **Fix (form):** ReportForm now uses the shared explicit `<Turnstile>` component (action="report", token via onToken state, resetKey bumps after failed attempts, submit via the shared `callApi` helper — byte-for-byte the CreateAccountForm wiring). When a site key is configured and no token exists yet, the form sends NOTHING and shows "The human check hasn't loaded yet — give it a moment, or reload the page if it doesn't appear." New `components/report/report-errors.ts` maps every API outcome to human copy (turnstile_required / turnstile_failed → human-check copy; validation_error → per-field hints; unknown_target, rate_limited, 5xx, network all mapped) — the raw API detail is never shown. Server side untouched: bot protection intact (missing token still 400 `turnstile_required`, bad token 403 `turnstile_failed`).
- **Fix (page):** `app/report/page.tsx` now mirrors `app/account/page.tsx` exactly — `const [sp, env] = await Promise.all([searchParams, getRuntimeEnv()])` and `siteKey={env.turnstileSiteKey ?? null}`.
- **Regression tests:** new `tests/unit/report-form.test.ts` (5 tests: SSR asserts the shared widget's mount markup and NO cf-turnstile/script; unit tests of the error mapper for every code incl. a no-echo assertion on raw detail). Guardrail in `tests/unit/guardrails.test.ts`: no component outside the shared widget may load api.js or use cf-turnstile/data-sitekey, all five forms must use the shared widget, and — added with the page fix — no app/component file may read `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` directly and the report/account/add-your-farm pages must source `turnstileSiteKey` via `getRuntimeEnv()` (the NEXT_PUBLIC_ fallback lives only in `lib/api/env-config.ts`). Both guardrails confirmed to FAIL against the pre-fix code.
- **Verify:** `npm run verify` green (ESLint + tsc clean, Vitest **346/346**, 20 files — 340 baseline + 6 new). Quinn gate on the form fix: **PASS** (diff vs CreateAccountForm wiring, server files untouched, error-map coverage, independent test runs incl. negative guardrail test). The page key-sourcing fix is a 3-line mirror of the proven account-page pattern, coordinator-reviewed, guardrail-covered, and proven by the live HTML below.
- **Deploy:** Worker **`47393cbc-3045-4c7e-85d8-3d8dcc48ac41`** (Tessa's recipe: cf:build, wrangler from a neutral directory via `tmp/cf-run.py`). Supersedes the intermediate `43e6838f` (form wiring only).
- **Live verification:** `/report` → **200** with the shared widget's container (`min-h-[65px]`) and the correct helper note ("A quick check from Cloudflare confirms you're a person…"); the "isn't set up" note, the legacy `cf-turnstile` div, and any inline api.js are all ABSENT from served HTML. `POST /api/v1/reports` without a token → **400 `turnstile_required`** (enforcement intact; the probe created no report/review rows). Spot checks: `/seller/select-seeds`, `/seller/songbird-seed-co`, `/seller/the-gardeners-workshop` all 200.
- **Remaining proof (needs a real device):** a valid Turnstile token cannot be minted via curl, so the literal tap-through submission is Dustin's iPhone retest — the identical shared widget already serves the other four forms in production, and /report now receives the same site key they use. No throwaway test report was submitted, so no production review rows needed cleanup.
- **Follow-up (awareness, out of scope):** the four sibling forms still display `callApi`'s raw `r.message` directly (pre-existing, Quinn note) — worth a later consistency pass to route them through human-copy mappers like report-errors.ts.

## 2026-11-19 — Elevation art: state-flower plates on seller + state pages (LIVE)

The 16 finished state-flower plates (`artwork-states/`, previously unused by any page) now appear in two places, per Elena's art-elevation consult proposal 2 — no new painting. Spec + Ada ruling + both Quinn gates in one file: `specialist-consults/state-plates-placement-spec.md`.

- **Design (Nadia, UX):** one shared pattern — a `StatePlate` component + `lib/data/state-plates.ts` table (mirrors `STATE_SCENES`, keyed by slug, exactly the 16 painted states). On `/seller/{slug}` the plate sits inside the existing `<header>` after the summary content; on `/state/{slug}` it rests in the header beside the intro/h1/CTA — NOT floated beside the farm-list grid (would break the 1/2/3-col SellerCard grid and leave a ragged rail beside a paginated list). Frame is the existing `.art-frame`; image via `imageUrl('/artwork/states/{slug}.webp', { width: 640 })`, plain `<img>` at 1600×1600 attrs, shown whole; sizes 280px base centred / 300px sm / 320px lg. Seller plate eager, state plate lazy (hero is LCP). No caption (house rule held, no exception requested). Gating is data-only: the seller's already-resolved `stateByCode(s.region)` ∩ the plate table — no-plate states, no-region sellers, and non-US sellers render nothing; no substitute, no inference.
- **Accessibility (Ada): PASS WITH AMENDMENTS.** Plates are informative (WCAG 1.1.1) — alt pattern `{Flower}, {State}'s state flower` from the MANIFEST common names, with two amendments the bench built verbatim: Maine is "White pine cone and tassel, Maine's state floral emblem" (1 M.R.S. §211 designates a floral emblem, not a flower) and Minnesota is "lady slipper" with no apostrophe (MANIFEST's authoritative form). Bench requirements pinned in §A.5 (alts verbatim, no title/aria/role/link treatment, seller eager with fetchPriority OMITTED). **Still owed: Ada's §A.5.5 AT pass (screen reader / keyboard / 400% zoom on the built pages) — status remains "spec passes WCAG 2.2 AA review; built pages not yet AT-verified."**
- **Code (Claude Code bench):** new `lib/data/state-plates.ts`, `components/seller/StatePlate.tsx`, header changes in both page files (imports + wrapper + conditional figure only; hero untouched), new `tests/unit/state-plates.test.ts` (15 tests), a narrow guardrails allowlist addition, and an `acceptance.ts` fix — an old check forbidding `artwork/states/oregon.` on `/state/oregon` (leftover from the removed outline art) replaced with plate-presence checks plus a cross-seller plate check. One declared deviation, Quinn-ruled acceptable: React 19 auto-emits a default-priority preload for the eager seller plate (same as the existing hero); suppressing it would require the `fetchPriority="low"` Ada forbade. `npm run verify` green: lint + typecheck clean, **361/361 tests (21 files)** — run by the bench, by Quinn, and again by the coordinator on the shipped tree.
- **Gate (Quinn): PASS WITH NOTES** pre-deploy (all 16 mappings byte-identical to §A.2/MANIFEST; gating traps covered — Texas, region-null, non-US, name/location inference; local preview rendered + DOM-measured 280×280 @390px, 300 @768, 320 @1280, both pages, no 320px overflow) and **PASS WITH NOTES** live spot-gate (own production fetches; neighbours intact; acceptance failures ruled pre-existing/local-D1 class, incl. the rate-limit burst failing identically in the 2026-11-16 production run).
- **Deploy:** Tessa's recipe — `npm run cf:build` + `wrangler deploy` from a neutral directory via `v2/tmp/cf-run.py` — Worker code version **`febcca45-84bf-4894-a11c-a6f41d9018f0`** (rollback target `98fce777-e681-40fc-8e45-a2a64a4bf55a`). Revalidation via the rotated-secret precedent (fresh REVALIDATE_TOKEN, set via `wrangler secret put`, used once, never recorded): `seller:<slug>` ×27 + `list:seller` tags and 45 paths incl. all 16 `/state/<slug>` returned 200/revalidated.
- **Live verification (https://seeds.hues.flowers):** full sweep of all 27 seller pages + all 16 state pages — exactly one plate `<img>` per painted page, always the page's own state, alt exact (incl. Maine's emblem wording on Johnny's); David's Garden Seeds and Seedtime (no region on file) show no plate and no `artwork/states/` reference; state heroes unchanged (1200w scene, eager, high priority); plate CDN URLs 200 image. ## 2026-11-19 — UX elevation P1: route loading skeletons (LIVE)

The six browse routes now show a skeleton shaped like the real page while loading, per Nadia's build spec `specialist-consults/ux-elevation-build-spec-2026-11-19.md` §1 — no new painting, no copy change. Gate: `specialist-consults/ux-elevation-quinn-gate-2026-11-19.md` (P1 PASS after the seller-geometry fix; Ada `specialist-consults/ux-elevation-ada-review-2026-11-19.md` PASS WITH NOTES, one-line reduced-motion fix applied).

- **Design (Nadia, UX):** server-only kit `components/ui/Skeleton.tsx` (geometry only — a `loading.tsx` can never know a name, count, or query) + `loading.tsx` for `/variety`, `/variety/[slug]`, `/crop`, `/crop/[slug]`, `/search`, `/seller/[slug]`. Whole skeleton tree `aria-hidden` with exactly one sr-only `role="status"` line per route ("Loading varieties…" etc.); skeletons stay invisible ~200 ms via pure CSS (`.skeleton-delayed`, 160 ms reveal) so fast navigations never flash. No images, links, buttons, or focusable elements in any skeleton. `WaitlistBlock`'s loading branch got the same treatment ("Loading waiting-list status…").

The other two placements are untouched: the home hero → "Browse by crop" break and the waitlist success-moment variant. The MotifDivider component and the R2 asset stay in place for them.

This removal therefore takes out the whole slot, thin rule included; whether the old thin rule should return is Dustin's separate call, not assumed here.

At build and deploy time origin/main was still `48b746c` (the badge build still uncommitted in the main tree), the branch contained that tip in full, and the change touches no `app/page.tsx` or `app/about/page.tsx` — so no badge-build page copy was reverted (none had shipped). ## 2026-11-19 — Illustration composition standard REVISED (Dustin, voice call ~15:12–15:15 PST) + audit/repaint programme opened

Dustin, reviewing illustrations on the call: new varieties mean new artwork; current illustrations show stems going directly into the ground, which "may not be natural"; and flower detail matters — "if the whole plant is too big then we need to not have the whole plant in view."

- **Revised standard (recorded in THEME-ART-DIRECTION.md §4 and design-queue/QUEUE.md binding rules):** flower detail is the priority — the bloom must read clearly at card size. The whole plant is NOT required in frame; if the whole plant would shrink the flower, compose closer (partial plant, flowering stem section, or cut-stem view). Stems must never poke straight out of bare ground: where the base is in view, paint the natural growth habit (basal foliage, crown, branching, leaf nodes); where a closer composition excludes the base, that is preferred over an unnatural ground-line. Handling rules unchanged: 1120×2240, padded onto paper, shown whole, never cropped/stretched; Elena's painterly style, photographic reference, listed-variety check all unchanged.
- **Standing pairing opened:** every variety the scout adds gets its illustration painted under this standard (first queue: the 9 scout adds of 2026-11-19 — Snapdragon Potomac Dark Pink/Lavender/White/Yellow/Red, Statice QIS Dark Blue/Lavender/White/Yellow).
- **Existing-art audit opened:** all live variety + crop illustrations are being audited for the ground-poke defect (stem straight into bare ground, no natural base); affected count to be reported before any mass repaint; repaints in Quinn-gated batches, worst/most-visible first.

Dustin picked Direction A — Quiet Ledger (2026-11-20) from the 2026-11-19 design phase (`card-redesign-2026-11-19/DIRECTIONS.md` + `a-quiet-ledger.html`). Built in isolated worktree `~/workspace/seeds-hues-card-wt`, branch `card-quiet-ledger`, commit `f287a09` off origin/main `0440268` — the main tree's in-flight badge/filter build (`open-directory`, uncommitted) was never touched. **Not merged, not pushed, not deployed — HELD until the badge/filter build ships**, then: rebase onto new main, wire the real small-farm flag, re-run verify, Quinn re-gates the delta, Tessa ships, live-verify.

- **Build (7 files):** `ListingRow.tsx` full rebuild (surface card, sold-out steps down to paper; Fraunces 600 20px seller name loudest, underlined; status = dot + sentence-case word + friendly date via new `formatCheckedDate` in `lib/format.ts` — today/yesterday/"Mon D", no ISO anywhere; wheat-stem hairline via `.listing-stem-rule` using the already self-hosted `/theme/divider-stem.svg`; footer = "Report a problem" 13px link at 44px tap height + sage-tint 44px "Visit shop →" pill with the load-bearing 1.5px leaf border in every state, sold-out pill outline-only but working); variety page header gains "N shops · M in stock" + the honesty line directly under it (old lower wording removed — appears exactly once); in-stock sorts above sold-out (stable view-layer sort); seller page list becomes the same card stack; skeleton updated. 15 new component tests; `npm run verify` green — 376 tests (22 files), independently re-run by Quinn.
- **Badge integration point:** `PublicListing` on main has no small-farm field and there is no `SmallFarmBadge` on main — both land with the badge build. ## 2026-11-20 — Admin listing-remove endpoint SHIPPED (LIVE) + 7 directed retirements executed

Dustin's call (2026-11-20, options widget): build the endpoint rather than authorize one-off D1 writes. The endpoint closes the API gap recorded 2026-11-19: until today the only code path that could retire a single listing was a direct production-D1 write (listing 288 precedent).

- **Endpoint:** `POST /api/v1/admin/listings/:id/remove` (admin scope, in `ADMIN_ROUTES` beside the badge build's `sellers/:id/small-farm`). Body `{ reason?: string ≤500 }`. Semantics mirror `removeSeller` per listing: status → `removed`, one `listing_events` 'gone' row carrying the caller's reason, one `catalog_seq` bump, revalidate `/variety/<slug>` + `/seller/<slug>`. Unknown id → 404 `listing_not_found`; already-removed → 200 `already_removed: true` with zero writes. Concurrency is single-winner: one atomic batch whose seq bump and 'gone' insert are both gated on the listing still being live (`INSERT … SELECT … WHERE EXISTS`), guarded status flip with `RETURNING` last — a race loser writes nothing. Migration `0011_listing_event_reason.sql` adds the nullable `reason` column to `listing_events` (additive; `removeSeller`'s 'gone' events now record their reason too). Documented in `v2/README.md`.
- **History note:** the first dispatch of this task was recorded as stalled-before-start, but its worktree (`seeds-hues-remove-wt`) in fact held two finished commits (endpoint + a Quinn-gate concurrency fix). This run reviewed that code line-by-line, rebased it onto the shipped badge build (`f8f8dba`; conflicts in the two admin files were pure additive collisions, resolved keeping both sides), and carried it through a fresh gate — nothing was trusted on the earlier record alone.
- **Rebase defect found + fixed:** the first post-rebase verify failed 3 badge-build small-farm contract tests (varieties `small_farm` total 14→13) — the new contract test removed a shared seed listing earlier in the same file, shifting counts asserted later. Fixed in `e046baa`: the test inserts its own throwaway listing cloned from a seed row and removes that. Full `npm run verify` green: lint + typecheck clean, **399/399 tests (23 files)**.
- **Gate:** Quinn code review **PASS WITH NOTES** (record `v2/tmp/quinn-gate-listing-remove.md`) — auth (401/403 before handler), SQL-level idempotency/concurrency, migration compatibility, and the rebase resolutions all verified by her own inspection and test runs (81/81 on the two affected files); no Claude escalation needed. Notes are non-blocking: no 500/501-char reason boundary test (N2); `removeListing`'s initial SELECT doesn't filter `deletedAt` (N3, observation); the conditional seq bump duplicates `nextSeq()` inline by necessity (N4).
- **Deploy:** merge `cbb6486` on `main`, pushed. D1 Time Travel recovery point noted pre-migration (**2026-11-20T19:44:01Z**); migration 0011 applied to production D1 and the `reason` column read back. `cf:build`; `wrangler deploy` from a neutral directory via `v2/tmp/cf-run.py` — Worker version **`af3f3a95-d0dd-4ef2-a946-dc844895b3a4`** (rollback target `a34757eb`, the thin-stem ship). Pre-use live probe: no key → 401; admin key + unknown id → 404 `listing_not_found`.
- **Retirements executed through the endpoint** (each returned `already_removed: false`): High Country Gardens **1518, 1519, 1520, 1521, 1522, 1523** with reason `plants-not-seed` (perennial plant pages listed as seed), and Seedman **993** with reason `category-link` (pointed at the stocks.htm category page, no individual product page exists).
- **Quinn post-ship verification: PASS, all 5 checks** (record `v2/tmp/quinn-verify-listing-remove.md`): all 7 variety pages show no trace of the retired listings (`/go/<id>` count 0, seller name absent); `/go/<id>` returns 404 for all 7; control listing **1524** (HCG Swamp Milkweed, genuine seed) untouched — still on its page, `/go/1524` → 302, status `published`; production counts exactly **1,784 live** (1782 published + 2 active; removed 13 → 20); exactly one 'gone' event per retired listing with the correct reason.
- **Still live, awaiting Dustin's individual calls (NOT retired):** Johnny's wrong-variants 490 (Paper Daisy 'Pierrot') and 452 (Eucalyptus 'Baby Blue'); the breadseed-poppy 'Black Beauty' cluster 1354, 1355, 1374, 1375, 1496; TGW Stock 'Apricot' 381; and **listing 1617** — Marigold 'Sparkler' at Sow True Seed, whose product URL is `sowtrueseed.com/products/radish-sparkler` (a radish page), identified during this run from hunt run 13's flag. The endpoint now makes any of these a one-call retirement when Dustin rules.

- **Record (source of truth first):** `open-directory/seller-classification.md` — all 56 rows moved out of the DEFAULT section into the BADGE (24) and NO BADGE (32) sections with their judgments; the DEFAULT section now records the completed review (0 remaining). Mirrored in `v2/lib/catalog/small-farm-classification.ts` (BADGE 44, judged NO BADGE 40, DEFAULT empty, `SMALL_FARM_PRODUCTION_TARGET` 84/44/40); the apply script's recorded-count guards (`scripts/lib/small-farm-badges.ts`), its header, the `lib/catalog/assertions.ts` comments, and the `catalog-assertions` test pins were updated to match. - **Verification (production):** public API reads 84 sellers / 44 badged; all 24 flips badged, all 32 reviewed NO BADGE still unbadged, the original 20 BADGE and 8 judged NO BADGE untouched. Rendered seller pages spot-checked: badge present on Sweet Pea Gardens, Urban Farmer, Strictly Medicinal Seeds; absent on Fedco Seeds and Southern Seeds (both confirmed NO BADGE).
- **Quinn spot-check (read-only):** sampled 13 judgments — every scale-suggestive BADGE flip plus three NO BADGE calls that could have erred the other way. **11 confirmed. 2 DISPUTED (both BADGE flips she would call NO BADGE), returned here as a list per protocol — NOT silently flipped; both remain badged pending a ruling:**
  - **urban-farmer** — Quinn: its own About headlines 2,500+ varieties, 100,000+ customers annually worldwide, a 75+ grower network, an 8,000 sq ft building, and a wholesale program — a national aggregator/distributor; the volume exceeds Fedco's 60,000 orders (confirmed NO BADGE), and like Baker Creek the garage/founder origin is outweighed by current scale; it does not *clearly* look small.
  - **halden-garden** — Quinn: owner-operators Sara & Mitchell Haller are supported, but Halden's own About says the small seed shop "quickly grew into a large online nursery," it pivoted to education in 2023, sold its mum mother stock that year, and its current offer is a curated monthly box sourced from professional growers nationwide — self-described as large, no longer clearly a small farm/seed shop.
- **Hardest calls (besides the two disputes):** Fedco Seeds (NO BADGE — a cooperative, but ~150 workers / 60,000+ orders / $10M+ a year is national-institution scale, the Seed Savers pattern); Southern Seeds (NO BADGE — genuinely family-founded, now a 2M+-packet marketplace reseller sourcing from other farms); Outsidepride (NO BADGE — its "small, family-run" claim outweighed by bulk/wholesale scale; this is the badge Quinn's 2026-11-19 audit had disputed, now settled OFF on the evidence); Osborne Seed, Trailing Petunia, Everwilde Farms (NO BADGE — family/small claims outweighed by commercial-dealer, bulk-store, and bulk-bag presentations).

### BADGE — flipped live (24)

| Slug | Seller | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| `sweet-pea-gardens` | Sweet Pea Gardens | Husband-and-wife farm in Chehalis, WA growing all of its own sweet pea seed on its farm. |
| `uprising-seeds` | Uprising Seeds | Small family-run certified organic seed farm in Bellingham, WA growing most of its own seed. |

## 2026-11-20 ~16:49 PT — Seller types: tags + public multi-select filter (Dustin directive)
Dustin (voice call) worked out the seller-type taxonomy with Hue: **Grower, Breeder, Seed company, Wholesaler, Broker, Reseller, Cooperative, Nursery / garden center, Seed bank / preservation, Marketplace seller.** Rulings: (1) types are **tags** — a seller carries as many as fit (Johnny's = seed company + grower; Territorial = seed company + grower, own farm plus contracted/bought-in seed); one **primary type** is kept for sorting in the admin. (2) The **public** front end gets a seller-type filter where visitors select **as many types as they want, in combination** (multi-select). (3) The small-farm filter option is labelled **"Small farm"** (singular) — not "Small farms only". This feeds the admin sellers section (tag editing alongside the badge call) and a public filter build; neither is built yet — recorded here and in MEMORY.md for the build briefs.

### Correction (2026-11-20 ~16:51 PT, same call) — NO primary tag; filter lives on the varieties page
Dustin rejected the "primary type for sorting" idea Hue proposed — a seller appearing under several tags is fine. Final model: **pure tags, no primary**. The public filter sits on the **varieties page**: it starts at **All**, and each tag a visitor selects narrows the shown sellers down; tags combine freely. **"Small farm" is one of the tags in that filter**, and the seller badge stays as built.

### Addendum (2026-11-20 ~16:52 PT) — Small farm is the prestigious tag
Dustin: these are tags, not categories. The **Small farm** tag is the prestigious one — sellers carrying it go **at the top of the list**, and the tag shows as a small-farm tag on their seller profile (the badge as built).

Marshmallow (Althaea) is not a catalog crop and not a cut flower in this catalog's terms.

Finding: NOT a verifiable US seed seller. Evidence: (1) the storefront runs on Shopline (myshopline.shop); (2) its About page is generic dropship boilerplate ("We offer products that you might like and use, such as homes, clothing, accessories, etc."); (3) the homepage brands itself "Lush-Farm" while the store is "AngleTok"; (4) its homepage testimonials are copied from a different business — the Cheryl Jackson review appears twice (once garbled, once clean) and the clean copy thanks "Brijette's Masterclass" for seed-starting teaching; (5) no address, named operator, or business registration anywhere. It sells plantable seed-paper gift products plus some seed packets (e.g. Butterfly Flower $6.99) — a gift/novelty storefront that also lists seeds, not a seed seller. **Call for Dustin: do not publish anything from AngleTok; seller record removal is his call.**

## 2026-11-20 — Soft-404 PR #2 reviewed: DOES NOT FIX production — NOT merged; root cause found

The dashboard reconciliation sweep flagged PR #2 (branch `muse/44824333056`, opened 2026-11-20 14:14 PT, zero reviews) as stalled. Hue reviewed it line by line. **Verdict: do not merge — the PR cannot fix the defect.**

- **The diff is tests only** (+194/−0, 2 files): a new `v2/tests/unit/route-not-found.test.ts` (10 tests) and one added case in `v2/tests/integration/data.test.ts`. **Zero changes to any page or route code.** The seller/crop/state page files are byte-identical between main and the PR branch, and they already contain the supposedly missing mechanism: `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"`, the slug-regex guard, and `if (!s) notFound()` (seller page comment: "A removed seller answers 410 from middleware.ts; this notFound() is the backstop").
- **Verify is green on the PR branch** (Hue ran `npm run verify` in an isolated worktree: lint + typecheck clean, **410 tests passed**, including the new route-not-found suite). That is precisely the trap: the new tests render the page components in-process against a fake catalog and assert the thrown `NEXT_HTTP_ERROR_FALLBACK;404` digest — they cannot observe the HTTP status of a streamed response. The test file's own header concedes this ("the mechanism itself, which in-process rendering cannot see"); its route-config pins only assert that `force-dynamic`/`notFound()` strings are present, which was already true.
- **Production still soft-404s** (curl, 2026-11-20 ~21:36 PT): `/seller/definitely-not-a-real-seller-xyz` → **200**, `/crop/definitely-not-a-real-crop-xyz` → **200**; controls: bad state slug → **404**, bad variety slug → **404**, real seller (`/seller/geoseed`) → 200, real crop (`/crop/dahlia`) → 200. The bad-seller response is a fresh render (`Cache-Control: private, no-cache`), not an edge-cached artifact.
- **Root cause: the segment `loading.tsx` skeletons.** `app/seller/[slug]/loading.tsx` and `app/crop/[slug]/loading.tsx` exist (UX elevation P1, commits af1a785 / aa80a15); `app/state/[slug]/` has **no** loading.tsx — only `page.tsx`. On these force-dynamic routes, the shell (layout + skeleton) streams out with HTTP 200 before the page's async load throws `notFound()`, so the status can no longer become 404; the not-found UI streams into a 200 response. Smoking gun: the live bad-seller 200 body contains the skeleton markup ("Loading seller…", ×2) ahead of the "Seller not found" content; the bad-state 404 body contains no loading markup. Variety escapes the bug because it is ISR (its 404 is decided before streaming), not because its code differs.
- **The real fix** (not in PR #2, not yet built): stop the seller/crop responses from flushing before the not-found decision — e.g. remove or rework the two segment `loading.tsx` files to match the state route, accepting the skeleton-UX tradeoff as a design call — then prove it over real HTTP (`v2/scripts/acceptance.ts` already asserts GET /seller/does-not-exist → 404 and /crop/does-not-exist → 404). An in-process unit test must never again be the only gate for a status-code defect.
- **Paperwork:** PR #2 left OPEN and unmerged (its tests are valid regression coverage for the component-level behavior and can ride along with the real fix). Dashboard card `01M7Y9EWV8EZDQWN4G3NY0EA01` updated via the sync push path (`task.update`, dedupe `task-update:soft404-pr2-reviewed:2026-11-20`, now v3): retitled "Fix soft-404 on seller and crop pages — PR #2 reviewed: tests only, does not fix production", detail carries this finding; status stays in_review, blocked on Hue — the fix is still owed.

## 2026-11-20 — Filter-update motion SHIPPED LIVE (PR #10)
Dustin, on the shipped in-place filter: "add some visual animation so it's noticeable that the list updated." **Mina (UI Design) specified the treatment** (`design-queue/filter-update-motion-spec.md`): on a filter change only, the incoming seller rows **cascade in** — fade opacity 0→1 with a 6px rise, 220 ms per row on a new easing token `--ease-filter-settle: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)` (no overshoot), staggered 30 ms per row capped at 120 ms — **340 ms total**. No exit animation by design (two mounted lists would force layout movement); only opacity/transform animate, so nothing shifts during the motion; the trigger is the keyed remount (`key` by filter state) + the `listing-swap` class gated on the existing `filterTouched` flag, plus one added `setFilterTouched(true)` line in the popstate callback so Back/Forward animates identically. First load/SSR/hydration never animate (first-paint markup unchanged). **Reduced motion: animation removed entirely** via an explicit `animation: none` block — Mina flagged that the existing global rule collapses duration but not delay, which alone would have left a staggered blink.
**Build:** brief `muse-tasks/filter-update-motion.md` (Mina's spec verbatim; PR #9), Muse run 44871259977 (verify gate green in-workflow), PR #10 — exactly the three files the spec names (globals.css, SellerFilterSection.tsx, variety-seller-filter.test.ts; +121/−5). **Quinn build gate: PASS WITH NOTES** (~/workspace/seeds-hues/QUINN-GATE-filter-motion-pr10.md) — no defects at any severity; Quinn re-ran `npm run verify` herself: eslint + typecheck clean, **430 tests passed** (422 baseline + exactly the 8 new motion tests; no test deleted/skipped/weakened — the one modified pattern guard was re-pointed for the sanctioned popstate line and made stricter); the components-layer CSS block is 33/33 lines identical to spec §8; all 11 PR #8 criteria re-checked, no regression. Notes are non-blocking (whitespace-only token grouping; no executable DOM test for the hydrated cascade — node-only suite, sanctioned by the brief; deliberate spec-pinning guard tests).
**Merged:** PR #10 → main, merge commit `d19d4c8`. **Deployed:** Worker version `7e2f1eb4-4ddb-4a25-a14e-8c9ffea472f9` (cf:build in the variety-layout worktree + wrangler deploy from a neutral directory via cf-run.py; rollback target `0f4eb1fa`, the in-place filter ship).
**Live verification (curl, production):** /variety/shirley-poppy-mother-of-pearl → 200, and its server-rendered HTML contains **no** `listing-swap` class (first load unchanged, as specified). The live production CSS chunk (`/_next/static/chunks/1iggl1_3pg3g7.css`) contains the `listing-item-in` keyframes, the `.listing-swap` rules with `animation: listing-item-in var(--duration-filter-item) var(--ease-filter-settle) both`, both new tokens, and `translateY(6px)`. **Still Dustin's proof:** the motion's feel — noticeable-but-calm on his phone/iPad, the reduced-motion device pass, focus proof while the cascade runs, real-browser Back/Forward, and a long-list CLS spot-check — this VM's headless browser cannot reach production through Cloudflare. Dashboard card `01M7Y870F363RWC8ENKWSPM7Y8` updated via the sync push path (`task.update`, dedupe `task-update:inplace-filter-motion-shipped:2026-11-20`, now v4): status shipped, active cleared, detail records both ships + Worker versions.

## 2026-11-20 — Soft-404 on seller and crop pages FIXED FOR REAL (PR #11, LIVE)

The fix the PR #2 review above called for has shipped. **Design ruling (Nadia UX + Mina UI, unanimous): Option B — rework, do not remove.** Hoist the existence check so the 404 decision lands before any shell streams; demote the tuned skeletons to inner Suspense fallbacks; exactly one sr-only role="status" announcement per page; fallbacks reserve the original skeleton geometry; the pre-stream check reuses the same cache()-shared lookup (no new queries).

- **Build:** brief `muse-tasks/fix-soft-404-real.md` (rulings verbatim; PR #2's test files cherry-picked onto the task branch so they land with the fix), Muse run 44873065549 → PR #11. The builder restructured both [slug] pages (outer check-then-render + sync content components) and moved the skeleton trees verbatim into new `skeleton.tsx` files — but its sandbox lost all command execution (bubblewrap loopback failure) and it had no file-deletion tool, so it handed off the load-bearing deletions unexecuted and claimed no runtime results. The coordinator applied the two [slug] `loading.tsx` deletions to the PR branch.
- **Second layer found by local HTTP proof (coordinator):** the first completed build fixed seller (404) but bad crop still returned 200 — its streamed shell carried "Loading crops…", the **crop index** segment's skeleton. Per the bundled Next docs, a folder's loading.js wraps "the page.js file and any children below" in its Suspense boundary, so `app/crop/loading.tsx` was still flushing a 200 shell ahead of `/crop/[slug]`'s notFound(). Same Option B demotion applied to the crop index (tree verbatim → `app/crop/skeleton.tsx` as the index page's inner Suspense fallback; segment loading.tsx removed). Seller needed no equivalent — `app/seller/` has no index loading.tsx.
- **Quinn build gate: PASS WITH NOTES** (`~/workspace/seeds-hues/QUINN-GATE-soft404-real-pr11.md`) — no defects at any severity; she re-ran verify herself at the PR head (441/441), confirmed all three skeleton trees and all three content bodies byte-identical to the originals, exactly one LoadingShell per affected page, and PR #2's tests byte-identical to PR #2's branch (zero adaptations). One honest note: on the detail pages the content components are synchronous over the pre-loaded data, so their Suspense fallbacks are structurally present but practically unreachable — detail pages now behave like the state pages (no interim skeleton); the crop-index fallback is genuinely reachable. Conditions (1)–(3) hold; Ada's floor holds.
- **Real-HTTP evidence (local preview builds, same seed):** PR build — bad seller 404, bad crop 404, real seller 200, /crop/dahlia 200, bad variety 404, bad state 404, /crop 200; zero skeleton markup in either 404 body. Acceptance suite: PR build **130/140** vs unmodified baseline (d19d4c8) **127/140** — the delta is exactly the two target checks flipping FAIL→PASS ("GET /seller/does-not-exist → 404", "GET /crop/does-not-exist → 404"); all 10 remaining PR failures are an exact subset of the baseline's, pre-existing (variety pagination/redirect fixtures, hunt-changed stock fixtures, Oregon plate count, the stale "approves by hand" and divider-stem assertions, local-media fixtures). Baseline curls reconfirmed the defect (bad seller/crop → 200). Quinn also flagged, out of scope: `GET /variety?page=99 → 200` fails identically on baseline — a separate task if wanted.
- **Merged:** PR #11 → main, merge commit `8f75b9b`. PR #2 closed as superseded (its tests landed byte-identical inside PR #11). **Deployed:** Worker version **`9d0a34fc-1e8b-4ce1-aa0a-2d06d1013671`** (cf:build in the soft404 worktree on merged main + wrangler deploy from a neutral directory via cf-run.py; rollback target `7e2f1eb4`, the filter-motion ship).
- **Live verification (curl, production):** /seller/definitely-not-a-real-seller-xyz → **404**, /crop/definitely-not-a-real-crop-xyz → **404**, /seller/geoseed → 200, /crop/dahlia → 200, bad variety → 404, bad state → 404, /crop → 200; neither 404 body contains skeleton markup; both contain their not-found content. The soft-404 defect is closed.

## 2026-11-21 — Quinn daily audit gains a catalog-wide DUPLICATE-LISTING check

At Dustin's direction (2026-11-21, after the Basil 'Cinnamon' page showed 24 listing cards for 17 real sellers), Quinn's daily sample audit (cron `seed-directory-quinn-daily-audit`, daily 18:55 PT) now carries a catalog-wide, every-run DUPLICATE-LISTING CHECK. The audit previously sampled for wrong-crop and bad-link defects but never checked for same-seller duplicate listings — the class that produced the Cinnamon duplicates (one seller's packet-size variants, another seller's product-vs-collection URL pair, each published as separate listings). The check: from the live production snapshot, group all LIVE listings by (seller, variety) pair; any pair with more than one live listing is a CRITICAL-class finding (duplicate listings corrupt the public seller and listing counts), reported in the findings with the seller name, the variety name, every listing id in the pair, and every product URL. It is NOT window-bound and NOT sample-bound — it covers the whole live catalog on every run, including pairs published before the audit window. A clean run must state "duplicate-listing check: 0 pairs" explicitly, so the check's presence is provable from the report itself. Applied via cron.update with the complete revised body — schedule (daily 18:55:47 America/Los_Angeles), scope, read-only posture, state-file behavior (quinn-audit-state.json), and the rest of the reporting all preserved; the only other change is the REPORT section naming the new check's result. Verified by reading the saved job back with cron.view: the new section and the report clause are present, nothing else changed. The audit job was NOT run as part of this update; the check first executes at the next scheduled run (Sat 2026-11-21 18:55 PT).
