## 2026-11-21 — Testing coverage audit + standing testing standard + Quinn usability training

Commissioned by Dustin after the admin sign-in form shipped broken in real browsers while every automated check passed ("Make sure that testing includes all things that need to be tested"). Full record: `~/workspace/seeds-hues/testing-audit-2026-11-21/` (COVERAGE.md, STANDARD.md, BROWSER-PASS-SPECS.md). The BusyForm fix itself is a separate workstream; this entry is the audit of everything else.

- **The structural finding:** the repo's only test runner is Vitest in the node environment (38 files, 718 `it()` blocks) — no jsdom/happy-dom, no Testing Library, no Playwright/Puppeteer anywhere. Every component test is `renderToStaticMarkup` + string assertions; **no test anywhere constructs a `FormData`**; every form-equivalent test hand-builds a JSON `Request` and calls a handler. The entire browser layer — form construction, events, client state machines, history/focus, Turnstile, service workers — is structurally unreachable by the suite, not weakly covered. The handler/integration layer itself is genuinely strong (real D1 + migrations, hand-computed expectations, non-vacuous privacy proofs); the failure is a missing layer, not weak tests.
- **Matrix totals (COVERAGE.md):** 30 user-facing flows inventoried (10 public browse, 9 public forms/accounts, 11 admin). **All 30 carry an unproven browser layer; 18 are critical or major GAPs** — 12 critical: variety-page in-place seller filter, create account, visitor sign-in, waitlist (signed out), report-a-problem, Add Your Farm, admin sign-in (the founding gap), review-queue decisions, seller edit sheets, listing retire/restore sheets, variety sheets, account role sheets; 6 major: search + autocomplete, code entry, waitlist (signed in), account panel actions, admin sign-out, Sheet/ConfirmDialog chrome. A whole untested surface class: the eight admin FormData route handlers (`sign-in/request|verify|sign-out`, `review/decide`, `sellers|listings|varieties|accounts/actions`) are invoked by **zero** tests — the BusyForm defect lived exactly in this class.
- **Ship-record corroboration:** across every QUINN-GATE / HUE-REVIEW / ship entry, no feature was verified by real-browser interactive use against production; gates repeatedly state the VM's headless browser cannot reach production through Cloudflare and name Dustin's iPad eyeball as the final proof. Sole local exception: the 2026-11-19 spot-paintings ship used local Playwright DOM measurement and drove Add Your Farm/account error states locally. The report form's Turnstile tap-through remains Dustin's iPhone retest, owed since 2026-11-18.
- **STANDARD.md (proposed standing standard):** "verified" is defined per flow type — anything a user submits requires a DOM-submission or real-browser test of the actual submit path (hand-built JSON does not count) plus Quinn's human pass; handler/curl proof is explicitly insufficient for interactive flows; admin write routes must be invoked by at least one test each; boundaries (Turnstile, credentials, email) are declared, never rounded up.
- **Quinn playbook amendment (Dustin's rulings, 2026-11-21):** `~/workspace/custom-skills/specialist_quality_assurance/SKILL.md` now carries, in "Audit web pages and interactive work", the human-interaction and usability pass as a named, non-waivable gate step for interactive work — with the BusyForm escape as its founding example — plus the rule that an interactive PASS never rests on the suite alone (unrun pass = declared partial), and a matching confirm line in her verification checklist.
- **Quinn deep usability training (same playbook, same day):** a full new discipline section, "Usability testing — the discipline behind the human pass" (playbook 83,523 → 101,294 bytes), researched from authoritative public sources actually read: NN/g (10 heuristics; severity ratings; usability testing 101; think-aloud; heuristic evaluation; error-message, form, and confirmation-dialog guidelines), GOV.UK Design System (error message/summary, validation, question pages), Baymard public articles (adaptive validation errors, multicolumn forms, filter UI, 8 search query types — paid interior NOT read and not cited), 18F Methods via its public mirrors + Digital.gov, IxDF public articles. Contents: methods and when each applies; how to run a task-based session (goal in the user's words, think-aloud log, friction in order, unhappy paths, stop points as results); the Nielsen 0–4 severity scale with the house gating mapping (severity 4 or a blocked core task = FAIL; severity 3 caps at PASS WITH NOTES with written lead acceptance); the gate report's required usability-section format; per-flow-type checklists (forms — incl. the BusyForm lesson and the GOV.UK-vs-NN/g validation-timing divergence, flagged as test-against-the-claimed-standard; filters/search; sign-in/account; admin queues/edit sheets; confirmation dialogs; cards/lists); viewports per house rules (phone-first public, iPad-first admin). usability.gov itself could not be retrieved in the research pass and is honestly not cited as read.
- **Browser passes — specified, NOT run by this workstream:** the audit agent cannot operate a live browser. BROWSER-PASS-SPECS.md specifies seven human-style passes for a browser-capable agent (search + autocomplete; in-place filter incl. Back/Forward and reduced motion; /go/ click-through; report form to the Turnstile boundary; waitlist to the code step; Add Your Farm validation; admin flows once a tester admin session exists), each with goal, steps, expected results, usability-observation prompts, honest stop points, and test-data cleanup. Results belong in `BROWSER-PASSES.md` in the audit folder; until they run, the 18 critical/major GAPs remain open by the standard's own terms.
- **Defects found by the audit:** none new beyond the documented structural gaps — the audit's evidence is the coverage record itself (untested route class, unproven browser layer, owed device proofs). No code was changed in this workstream.

## AngleTok (seller id 59) — Dustin ruling 2026-11-21: retained in background as do-not-add marker, removed from public (executed 2026-11-21, Priya)

- **Ruling (Dustin, 2026-11-21, verbatim):** "We need to save it in the background so it doesn't get added in the future, remove from public." Background: Quinn's daily audit #2 finding F3 — AngleTok is NOT a verifiable US seed seller: its storefront brands itself 'Lush-Farm', dropship boilerplate, lifted reviews, no address/operator. Zero listings; none ever published. The record was NOT deleted.
- **Eligibility set:** `POST /api/v1/admin/sellers/59/reject` (admin-key API, sanctioned path only) → HTTP 200, `{"seller":"angletok","id":59,"eligibility":"rejected"}` at 2026-11-21T22:27:41Z. Exact state: **eligibility = rejected** (the permanently ineligible state; the `remove` / removed_by_request flow was deliberately not used). The endpoint's note field stored this note on the record (sellers.approvalNote): "F3 (Quinn daily audit #2): not a verifiable US seed seller - storefront brands itself 'Lush-Farm'; dropship boilerplate; lifted reviews; no address/operator. Dustin ruling 2026-11-21: permanently ineligible (rejected); record retained in background as do-not-add marker; removed from public. Zero listings, none ever published."
- **Seller-notes disposition:** NO sanctioned API-key path can write a Slice B `seller_notes` row — on origin/main the API-key route table has no seller-notes endpoint; notes are writable only through the session-authenticated admin UI (`POST /admin/sellers/actions`, action=add_note). No write was improvised. Full note text to file in the admin UI: "Internal note — AngleTok (seller id 59, slug angletok, domain angletok.com, homepage https://angletok.com/). Quinn daily audit #2 finding F3: NOT a verifiable US seed seller — its storefront brands itself 'Lush-Farm'; dropship boilerplate; lifted reviews; no address/operator. Dustin's ruling 2026-11-21 (verbatim: 'We need to save it in the background so it doesn't get added in the future, remove from public.'): record retained in the background as a permanent do-not-add marker, removed from public — do not delete, do not re-add, do not re-approve. Action taken 2026-11-21: eligibility set to rejected via POST /api/v1/admin/sellers/59/reject. Zero listings; none ever published."
- **Visibility proofs (before: it WAS public — sellers query, seller API 200, search hit, 1 sitemap entry; after):** (a) seller page `GET /seller/angletok` → HTTP 404, 0 "AngleTok" in body; (b) public API — detail and listings both 404 not_found, `?q=AngleTok` → [], full paginated sellers list = 83 with AngleTok absent (84 before; reconciles); (c) site search — `/api/v1/search?q=AngleTok` and `?q=Lush-Farm` both empty, search page HTML contains 0 "angletok"; (d) sitemap.xml fetched and grepped → 0 occurrences (was 1). Extra: sync snapshot carries 83 sellers, AngleTok absent. Post-change admin health: ok, 0 violations, 1,480 published listings checked.
- **Do-not-add exclusion:** No standing eligibility exclusion mechanism existed in crawl-daily/ (ledger.json and variety-scout-state.json have no seller-exclusion slot). Created **`crawl-daily/do-not-add-sellers.json`** — the file future seller-hunt and variety-scout runs must consult at run start; it records seller id 59, name AngleTok, slug angletok, storefront brand 'Lush-Farm', domain angletok.com, homepage https://angletok.com/, the F3 reason and the ruling. Also added `angletok` with policy=blocked to the registry the hunt tooling already reads, `crawl-daily/pipeline/seller-policies.json`, so fetch2.py skips it on sight. Server-side backstop: the retained rejected record makes any future `POST /api/v1/ingest/seller-candidates` for angletok.com fail 409 seller_exists naming eligibility rejected. Flag for Hue: add a "read do-not-add-sellers.json at run start" line to the hunt/scout cron bodies.

## 2026-11-22 — Seller location sweep + contact collection (SEO-2) RUN

Dustin approved the sweep in Bea audit ruling #2 and extended it the same day (voice call 15:10 PT): while sweeping, collect each seller's homepage URL, customer service phone, and customer service email from the seller's own website for the shop pages. Full record: `~/workspace/seeds-hues/seller-contacts-2026-11-22/REPORT.md`; dataset `seller-contacts.json` / `seller-contacts.csv` beside it (every value carries source URL + 2026-11-22 check date); raw evidence in that folder's `work/`.

**Location sweep.** All 83 live seller pages fetched: visible location, meta description, and PostalAddress schema agree with each other on 83/83 pages — all three derive from the single field, so a wrong field is wrong in three places at once and a corrected field fixes all three. Region/state-only fields render no PostalAddress. Against the sellers' own published facts: **5 contradictions** — GeoSeed (28) Clinton vs Greenwood, SC (fix reserved to Dustin's admin edit per ruling #2; verified, not touched); Osborne Seed (40) field "Salt Lake City, UT" vs the seller's own "PO Box 1647, Mount Vernon, WA 98273" on every page checked (its directory About repeats SLC); Outsidepride (32) Salem vs "1565 Stryker Road, Independence, OR 97351" (mailing + shipping, their support page); Vermont Wildflower Farm (75) Charlotte vs footer "Alburgh, VT 05440"; Plant Good Seed (65) Chimacum, WA vs the only location their site publishes today, mailing address "226 West Ojai Ave Ste 101-539, Ojai, CA 93023" (Chimacum appears nowhere on their site — flagged for Dustin's call, may be mailing-vs-farm). **2 empty location fields** with seller-published values ready: David's Garden Seeds (26) → Poteet, TX; Seedtime (27) → Nashville, TN. Peace Seedlings (12) is a specificity improvement, not an error (field "Willamette Valley, OR"; seller's own city is Corvallis, OR — also what its About says), as are 16 more region/state fields whose sellers publish a city. TRF-LA (Scarsdale = sister NY store) and J.L. Hudson ("Elmwood, NE" = a worked example in his instructions; he confirms La Honda and states he has no telephone) checked and cleared.

**Correction-path blocker (needs Dustin's route decision).** No sanctioned API route exists for seller location: the admin-key routes cover description, badge, and status only; location/homepage/contact-email are editable only via the admin UI behind a signed-in session. The sweep therefore applied NO corrections (and did not write D1 directly or abuse the visitor report form). Ready corrections wait on either Dustin's admin edits (same sitting as GeoSeed) or a small `sellers/:id/location` endpoint build on the Tranche 1.1/1.8 precedent.

**Contact collection.** Homepage: 83/83 recorded and live-verified (79 direct; Park Seed, High Mowing, Gertens, Peace Seedlings refuse this VM's fetches at their CDNs and were read via the crawl route). Customer phone: **49/83**; customer email: **48/83**; the rest are recorded "not published" with the stated reason (form-only contact, obfuscated email, or the seller's own "no telephone" statement) — never filled in. A 30-seller manual context pass caught the traps: four published numbers are faxes (Great Basin 435-283-6872, Territorial fax-orders 888-657-3131, Harris 877-892-9197, True Leaf 801-467-5730 — the automated pick had selected True Leaf's fax; corrected to its labelled phone 801-491-8700), and Strictly Medicinal's only number is a fax, so its phone is honestly unpublished. Schema note for the shop build: `contact_email` and `homepage` columns exist (private / no API writer), **no phone column exists** — the dataset file is the deliverable until the shop contact block is built.

## 2026-11-22 — Seller-location endpoint COMMISSIONED (Dustin ruling) — build dispatched

Dustin ruled 2026-11-22, verbatim: "Build the small location endpoint so the team can apply them" — route 2 of the sweep's correction-path decision. Scope: ONE new admin route, `POST /api/v1/admin/sellers/:id/location`, mirroring the shipped `sellers/:id/description` route exactly (admin-scope key, idempotent, the same `sellerUpdate` funnel as the admin UI so the audit trail and revalidation match a UI edit), with a validated `{ location }` body and the updated seller returned as read-back. One design point the brief settles: `region` is an independent column driving the state filter, state pages, and the StateShape icon, so the route derives region from the location's state and writes both in the same call (no derivable state → location only, region untouched). The build changes no production data. Brief `muse-tasks/seller-location-endpoint.md` (main `75be9dd`); pipeline run 45083700599 dispatched. Quinn gate to follow: auth refusals (no key / ingest-scope key / malformed key), validation problems, a single-purpose before/after proof (only location + derived region + mechanical audit fields may differ), idempotency, the region-derivation cases, non-vacuity both directions.

On the gate's PASS and ship, the coordinator applies the five sweep-verified fixes THROUGH the endpoint, each verified by read-back on the live seller page (visible location + meta description + PostalAddress schema — all three read from the one field): Osborne Seed (40) "Salt Lake City, UT" → "Mount Vernon, WA"; Outsidepride (32) "Salem, OR" → "Independence, OR"; Vermont Wildflower Farm (75) "Charlotte, VT" → "Alburgh, VT"; David's Garden Seeds (26) empty → "Poteet, TX"; Seedtime (27) empty → "Nashville, TN". EXCLUDED by the ruling: GeoSeed (28) — stays Dustin's own admin edit per his standing ruling; Plant Good Seed (65) — Chimacum, WA vs Ojai, CA is Dustin's call, being asked via Hue; nothing applied until he rules.

## 2026-11-22 — Location model RULED by Dustin (voice call 15:31 PT) — Plant Good Seed resolved; sweep findings re-read

Dustin, verbatim: "You know sellers have multiple locations so we could just list their multiple warehouse locations or locations just multiple locations. Or if it says a mailing address versus a physical address." — refined on the same call, governing: "And we need to combine all the locations to be the same location — just on the shop page we can have an information tab or area that has all of their location."

**The rule:** ONE primary location per seller everywhere in the directory (location field, state pages, schema — one canonical place; no multi-location data model, no multi-state filing). The full set of a seller's locations — physical / warehouse / mailing, each labelled by kind, from the seller's own published facts — lives ONLY on the seller's shop page, in an information tab or area. That element sits inside the SHOP design commission (PROGRAM.md SHOP item carries it as the LOCATIONS RULE with a delta design pass + Quinn delta gate). Full record: `seo-audit-2026-11-22/RULINGS.md`, addendum to ruling #2.

**Dispositions under the rule:**
- **Plant Good Seed (65): RESOLVED.** Primary stays "Chimacum, WA" — no endpoint write, no field change. The Ojai, CA mailing address (226 West Ojai Ave Ste 101-539, their own site) is shop-info-area data, recorded ready in the sweep dataset.
- **The five endpoint fixes proceed unchanged** (Osborne Seed → Mount Vernon, WA; Outsidepride → Independence, OR; Vermont Wildflower Farm → Alburgh, VT; David's Garden Seeds → Poteet, TX; Seedtime → Nashville, TN) — they are wrong-primary corrections, exactly what the single-field endpoint is for. The endpoint build (run 45083700599) and its Quinn gate are owned by the LOC coordinator; this ruling changes nothing in its brief.
- **The 17 specificity improvements** (Peace Seedlings + 16) stand as filed — not errors, no writes. Any additional published locations (mailing/warehouse) are shop-info-area material.
- **GeoSeed (28)** untouched, as standing — Dustin's own admin edit.
